```text
The Unimaginable

Quote 2

Vt

“[T]he unimagined is very different from the unimaginable. All that stands

between us and what we could imagine but never have, is novel

experience and / or the exercise of our powers of conceptual invention

and poetic expression. By contrast, to speak philosophically of the

unimaginable, unless by way of a harmless rhetorical flourish, is to invoke

a metaphysical boundary that can never be crossed. To do so in the
```

Session: Understanding and Reasoning in LLMs: How to Approach the Issue
Photo ID: AF1QipNBHIUukJeSb-d-kqG2adS_IeD6J6kwh632k4I4
Image: sources/slides/images/slide-153.jpg
Source image: https://lh3.googleusercontent.com/pw/AP1GczN3jKICNzAQ2CTK7uuAVXENdalIwGCeDh3YY3jwYA8uRJ3aJDu3edbBnXe0kkuV0S09CYvACer1P34pfLrDDf8Nqn2VLYvNP2lGbE-xddW_j3RTvWvs

Status: UNVERIFIED machine text from photographed material or a video preview. Inspect source image.

```text
Underatanding in Large Language Models

Paul Boghossian

NYU

The Question

(Question) Are LLMs germrinely intelligent, of are they just super-sophisticated,

super-fast pattern -matchets that simulate meelhgent behavior without being

intelligent?

Do LLMs solve problems in ways that ate similat to the way hurnans do so:

1

Understand the problem being posed.

bt

Desire to answet it and intend to answet it trathfully.

111

Reason about what it would take to answer it correctly.

Iv.

Know whethet the resources fot answering it ate already available to them

ot whether new evidence is required.

Vv.

_Aet on those beliefs, desires and intentions to develop an answer.

vi

Communicate that answet by fe//ing the interlocutor what the answer is.

(Q) Do LLMs solve problems the way humans do, ot are they just supet-

sophisticated, super-fast pattern-matchers that simulate human behavior?

Our Question, Conceptual Engineering and Apt Methodology

in its full human version. But the crucial

Perhaps what you say is true about understanding, say,

question that needs answering is not Q but rather Q*:

(Q*) Do Als instantiate the 4

are essence of these typical human capacities? It

might be interesting to ask wh

ether LLMs are able to instantiate the full human

but it’s even more important to ask whether they

!

version of, say, understanding,

are capable of understanding per se, even if it’s not in its

full human version — the

bare essence of understanding, as we may put it.

(Q**) Do they instantiate concepts of understanding that are specially engineered to

suit the new kind of intelligence they represent?

However, I would insist that the most responsible and fruitful way to approach these issues is to

start with asking about the full human case, that is, to start with Q itself.

The Main Arguments for the Opposing Views

Our Question has quickly become very polarizing,

Believers are convinced that LLMs understand, know, believe, desire, intend and reason (Hinton,

Capp

elen, Sutskever, Lederman). Skeptics (Marcus, Bender, Chomsky, Pearl, Pinker, Penrose)

who are just as convince

d that LLMs are simply very sophisticated, vety fast pattern-matchers.

In favor of the Believer’s thesis that LLMs have human-like mental capacities there is first and

foremost the fact that they are so impressive at what they can do. Best explanation is mentalistic.

What speaks in favor of the Skeptic’s position? Some Skeptics argue as follows:

i LLMs are at bottom stochastic next-token predictors.

ii. Such processes look nothing like belief, desire or intention.
```

```text
“5

THE WHOLE HOG ARGUMENT

How the network works

Understanding questions gives us reason to attribute knowledge of meanings. Knowledge brings belief

with it.

Answering questions gives us reason to attribute actions. Those actions provide evidence of intentions

and goals.

Selecting answers and developing plans provide evidence of reasoning.

These are familiar evidential transitions. Students’ answers indicate what they know; their explanations

provide evidence of how they reasoned.

We needn't treat every connection as metaphysically necessary.

ye

¥

Ps

hj

The network supports defeasible inferences. A particular transition can be challenged, but rejecting it requires

attention to the evidence,
```

```text
OBIECTIONS TO THE METHODOLOGY

Nuanced Detectors

ee

_—___

For those who resist the starting point, we offer a further argument.

Nuanced Detectors

ff

When large numbers of competent observers converge, after extended interaction in a variety of

circumstances including interactions intended to check for trickery, on a verdict that some object

l=

has mental states, that convergence is strong but defeasible evidence that it genuinely has mental

states.

The relevant judgments are ordinary: the system understood the request or noticed a contradiction.

We aren’t claiming that everyone endorses our philosophical view (the Whole Hog Thesis).
```

```text
OBIECTIONS TO THE METHOOOLOGY

Objection: We are engaged in pretense

—_

Ina children’s game, a stick becomes a sword. If it breaks, the children can decide whether the sword

has broken or whether the shorter stick still represents an Intact sword. The mapping between object

and pretense is negotiable.

ond

Spain,” we don't freely decide that it has instead asked about our favourite film.

The proposed pretense needs to explain this persistent constraint.
```

```text
Reply: Normal for us need not be norma

tal conditions. We normally

Human cognitive capacities developed under particular environmen

encounter convex faces. A concave mask can produce a systematic vi

sual illusion without showing that

we lack vision.

LLMs have a different developmental history. Their training may establish different conditions under

which their capacities work reliably. A task that is easy and familiar for us may place an LLM outside

those conditions.

The sceptic cannot simply move from “a human should get this right” to “an LLM that gets this wrong

»

lacks the capacity.”
```

```text
The general structure

An LLM cannot have feature F because it lacks something necessary for F.
```

```text
NECESSARY CONDITIONS AND THE GAME OF LACKS

Objection: Text alone cannot provide grounding

L

The grounding objection says that an LLM encounters symbols rather than the things those symbols

concern.

But those symbols are produced through human interactions with the world. The training corpus has a

causal history connecting it to objects and events.

We can think about Plato without having encountered him. We can talk about places we have never

visited.

Externalist theories help explain those capacities.

The absence of direct acquaintance doesn’t establish the absence of reference.

ty

=

-- -

ee

a

_

- —

A grounding objection needs to explain why the relevant mediated connections fail in the LLM case.

—
```

```text
refutes neither approach.
```

```text
SEVEN REPLIES TO PAUL

Reply 3: “Easy errors” don’t establish the absence of

——

——_.

—

pam e arta ee

understanding

Paul’s multiplication exchange shows unreliable calculation. His further claim is that performance

limitations cannot explain it because LLMs have massive computing power.

7

Speed and scale don’t establish reliable access to the information needed for a task. Recall the difficulty

of tracking successive block positions.

"i

Nor is human ease a neutral standard. Recall the hollow-mask illusion and the altered river-crossing

(3

2

puzzle.

Our position doesn’t depend on every elementary error eventually disappearing.

tO

=]

4

The issue is what explains the pattern of performance. An incorrect answer doesn’t itself distinguish absent

understanding from an unsuccessful exercise of understanding.
```

```text
SEVEN REPLIES TO PAUL

Reply 4: The plus/quus question needn

a node

Paul asks what could determine that a particular LLM node instantiates plus rather than quus.

Hyper-exter

nalism: look beyond the node: to the mathematical practice reflected in training and the system’s

subsequent use.

Recall Alex and Beth. Different histories can help determine different governing rules despite matching Internal

procedures.

The possibilities aren’t exhausted by biological evolution or a designer explicitly assigning a rule to a node.

This {sn’t a completed solution to infinitary content. More work hard work to be done.
```

```text
SEVEN REPLIES TO PAUL

Reply 5: The Justificatory Requirement needs engagement

Soe

with positive evidence

Paul distinguishes acting for a reason from merely conforming to a regularity. We agree that the

distinction matters.

Hyper-externalism alone doesn’t settle this further question.

But the marble exchange provides evidence of responsiveness to reasons. An LLM can also explain an

answer by setting out considerations that support it.

Such explanations can mislead. Our book explicitly acknowledges that they might not reveal the process

producing the answer. The same possibility exists with humans.

i: -

|

—

Set

—_

_

Se)

Paul needs to assess this positive evidence. The presence of a computational mechanism doesn’t establish that

reasons play no role.
```
